After a particularly heated argument at home where her spouse expressed fear about how much she had \"changed\" and insisted something was wrong beyond normal stress, Alice agreed—reluctantly at first—to see a mental health professional at the recommendation of their pastor and primary care doctor.\n- Chief Complaint: “I keep getting stuck on these ideas that people around me are plotting or judging me, even when they swear they aren’t.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): At the shop and even at church, I get convinced people are whispering about me or setting me up to fail—like when my supervisor moved me off a big EV job, I was sure he and my husband had talked and decided I shouldn’t be trusted around certain cars, and no matter how many times they both tell me that’s not true, I can’t shake the feeling there’s this plan going on behind my back.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): Every so often, especially late at night in the garage or when I’m alone in the house, I hear this man’s voice clear as day commenting on what I’m doing—saying things like, “They’re laughing at you,” or “You’re wasting your time,” even though I know there’s nobody actually there talking to me.\n- S023 (Negative_Symptoms): Compared to how I used to be—organizing meets for the car club and always messing with some project—now on a lot of days I just sit on the couch after work staring at the TV, not really caring about hanging out with friends or even finishing the old Camaro in the driveway, and my wife has to push me just to shower or answer texts back.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): My mood has been all over the place—some days I’m on top of the world and talking a mile a minute about baby names and new ideas for the shop, and then out of nowhere the smallest comment from my wife or a coworker will set me off and I’ll snap at them like they’re attacking me, and only later do I realize I overreacted.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): When I’m in one of those up phases, I can’t sit still at all—I’ll stay at the garage after my shift ends, start three different repair projects, reorganize all my tools, and then come home and decide to deep-clean the kitchen at one in the morning because I feel this constant push to keep doing something.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): I get these stretches where I honestly believe I’m the only one at the shop who really understands the new EV systems and that I’m meant to change how the whole place runs, like management should just hand me the training program and let me run it because I “see the bigger picture” that everyone else is too small-minded to get.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): There have been weeks where I’ll sleep maybe three or four hours a night, lying there wide awake with my mind racing about work and the baby we might have one day, but I still jump out of bed feeling wired and tell my wife I don’t really need sleep because I’ve got too much to do anyway.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): My wife and my coworkers keep telling me that lately, when I get going about a new project or some idea I have about people watching me, I talk so fast and jump from one thing to another that they can’t get a word in, and even when they try to interrupt I feel this pressure in my chest to keep talking until I’ve gotten it all out.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- psychotic_symptoms_and_manic_episode_occur_concurrently: When I first started staying late at the shop full of energy, barely sleeping and taking on extra EV projects, that was also when the voice really kicked in and I became convinced my coworkers and my wife were working together to undermine me.\n- manic_episode_is_present_for_the_majority_of_the_total_duration_of_the_active_and_residual_portions_of_the_illness: Looking back over this past year or so, most of the time I’ve either been in those wired, overconfident phases where I’m nonstop busy and talking fast, or just coming down from them, with only short stretches where my mood feels more normal.\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_for_2_or_more_weeks_in_the_absence_of_a_major_mood_episode: There was a stretch this past spring when my mood had mostly flattened out and I wasn’t especially up or down, but for a few weeks I still kept hearing that man’s voice and was sure people at work were plotting against me even though I wasn’t in one of my high-energy phases.\n- functional_impairment: Because of all this, my supervisor cut back my responsibilities after I argued with him about being sabotaged, my wife and I barely talk without fighting, and I’ve stopped going to most car‑club events or keeping up with basic things at home like bills and cleaning.\n- duration: All of this really started to build up about a year and a few months ago when we began seriously planning for a baby and I took on the extra EV training work at the shop.\n- psychotic_symptoms_duration: The voice and the strong feeling that people are plotting against me have been hanging around most of the time since late last fall.\n- manic_episode_duration: The periods where I’m revved up, sleeping very little, and throwing myself into project after project have been coming and going for close to a can’t get a word in, and even when they try to interrupt I feel this pressure in my chest to keep talking until I’ve gotten it all out.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- psychotic_symptoms_and_manic_episode_occur_concurrently: When I first started staying late at the shop full of energy, barely sleeping and taking on extra EV projects, that was also when the voice No, they usually happen at separate times rather than at the same time. When I'm in those "up" phases, I might get really irritated or snap at people, but I don't feel sad or hopeless until I crash into those low periods.

For instance, when my supervisor took me off a big EV job, I became convinced he and my husband had teamed up and decided I couldn't be trusted, and I still can't shake that feeling even though they both swear it's not true.\n\nDoctor: Besides these thoughts about others plotting against you, have you ever heard voices, seen things, or had other sensory experiences that others didn't seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do sometimes hear a man's voice, especially when I'm working late in the garage or alone at home. It comments on what I'm doing, saying things like \"They're laughing at you\" or \"You're wasting your time.\" Even though I hear it clear as day, I know there isn't actually anyone there.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing this voice, and how often does it happen during the week?\n\nPatient: It’s been going on since late last fall, so for about a year now. I hear it every so often, maybe a few times a week, mostly when I'm working by myself or alone in the house.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have you experienced any periods of feeling very down and depressed, or on the other hand, unusually energetic and hyperactive?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've experienced both extremes over this past year. I'll go through these highly energetic \"up\" phases where I feel wired, stay up late working on projects, and can't sit still, but then I'll crash into low phases where I just sit on the couch staring at the TV and struggle to even shower.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear these voices or have these paranoid thoughts even during times when your mood feels completely normal, or do they only happen when you are in those 'up' or 'down' phases?\n\nPatient: Yes, I actually do experience them even when my mood is completely normal. This past spring, my mood had flattened out and I wasn't feeling especially up or down, but for a few weeks I still kept hearing that man's voice and felt convinced people at work were plotting against me.\n\nDoctor: When you have these 'up' and 'down' phases, do they usually happen at separate times, or have you ever experienced them both at the same time—like feeling extremely energetic and wired while also feeling deeply sad or hopeless?\n\nPatient: No, they usually happen at separate times rather than at the same time. When I'm in those \"up\" phases, I might get really irritated or snap at people, but I don't feel sad or hopeless until I crash into those low periods.\n\nDoctor: How long do these 'up' and 'down' phases typically last? Do they stay the same for weeks or months, or do they rapidly switch back and forth within a few days?"
  }

The "up" phases definitely last for weeks at a time rather than shifting back and forth in just a few days. These periods have been coming and going like that for close to a year now.

Do they stay the same for weeks or months, or do they rapidly switch back and forth within a few days?\n\nPatient: The \"up\" phases definitely last for weeks at a time rather than shifting back and forth in just a few days. I'll have weeks where I barely sleep for more than three or four hours a night but still feel completely wired and energized. These periods have been coming and going like that for close to a year now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely energetic and wired, but at the exact same time felt deeply sad, hopeless, or empty, lasting for at least several days?"

No, I haven't really experienced feeling wired and deeply sad or hopeless at the exact same time. My high-energy periods and my low, unmotivated periods usually seem to happen at completely different times.

My high-energy periods and my low, unmotivated periods usually seem to happen at completely different times.\n\nDoctor: To help me rule out other potential causes, do you regularly use alcohol, recreational drugs, or take any prescription medications?\n\nPatient: No, I don't regularly drink alcohol or use any recreational drugs. I'm also not taking any prescription medications right now.\n\nDoctor: During those \"up\" phases when you feel wired and barely sleep, do you also experience things like your thoughts racing incredibly fast, talking much faster than usual, or taking unusual risks?\n\nPatient: Yes, my mind races constantly during those times, especially when I'm lying awake at night. My wife and coworkers also tell me that I talk incredibly fast and constantly jump from one topic to another. I don't really feel like I take dangerous or unusual risks, but the racing thoughts and fast talking are definitely very prominent.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over this past year, would you say these high and low mood phases have been present for most of the total time you've been dealing with these struggles, or just a small part of it?"

Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event. Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.
